## GHSA-7gwp-5pfp-969j · mlflow

2026-08-17 · Python · **CRITICAL** 9.3 · Pattern: `RACE_CONDITION→TOCTOU`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from a Time-of-Check-Time-of-Use (TOCTOU) race condition in the webhook URL validation. While `_validate_webhook_url` checked if a resolved IP was public, the subsequent `requests.post` call re-resolved the hostname independently. This allowed a DNS rebinding attacker to return a public IP during validation and then a private or link-local IP (e.g., 169.254.169.254) at the time of the actual request.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could achieve full-read Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF), allowing them to access internal services or cloud metadata endpoints (like AWS EC2 metadata service) by bypassing the initial URL validation.

### Key diff

```
--- a/mlflow/webhooks/delivery.py
+++ b/mlflow/webhooks/delivery.py
@@ -88,8 +88,14 @@ def _create_webhook_session() -> requests.Session:
         **extra_kwargs,
     )
 
-    adapter = HTTPAdapter(max_retries=retry_strategy)
+    adapter = SSRFProtectedHTTPAdapter(max_retries=retry_strategy)
     session = requests.Session()
+    # Disable env proxy handling: a proxy would make the validated peer the
+    # proxy rather than the webhook destination, bypassing the SSRF check.
+    session.trust_env = False
     session.mount("http://", adapter)
     session.mount("https://", adapter)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `SSRFProtectedHTTPAdapter` which overrides the connection classes to include a check for public IP addresses immediately after the socket connects, but before any TLS handshake or HTTP data exchange. This closes the TOCTOU gap by ensuring the peer IP of the actual connected socket is validated, preventing DNS rebinding attacks. Additionally, it disables environment proxy handling to ensure the SSRF check is not bypassed by proxies.

## GHSA-2qvg-qr73-mqxp · wktk/conflibot

2026-08-17 · YAML · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application was executing Git commands by directly concatenating user-controlled input (specifically, pull request branch names) into a shell command string. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary shell commands by crafting a malicious branch name.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary commands on the runner machine where the GitHub Action was being executed, leading to full compromise of the environment and potential access to sensitive data or resources.

### Key diff

```
-import { exec } from "child_process";
+import { execFile, spawn } from "node:child_process";
...
-  private system(command: string): Promise<[string, string]> {
-    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
-      exec(command, (error, stdout, stderr) => {
+  private git(...args: string[]): Promise<string> {
+    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
+      execFile(
+        "git",
+        args,
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces the use of `child_process.exec` with `child_process.execFile` and `child_process.spawn`. This change ensures that Git commands are executed directly with an array of arguments, preventing shell interpretation of user-controlled strings and eliminating the command injection vulnerability.

## GHSA-6x2c-phff-wx57 · github.com/QuantumNous/new-api

2026-08-17 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `CREDENTIAL_LEAK→LOG_EXPOSURE`

### Root cause

The application was logging sensitive information, specifically authorization tokens (bearer tokens) and potentially other request details, directly into debug logs using `fmt.Sprintf` without proper redaction or sanitization. This allowed an attacker with access to the logs to retrieve these tokens.

### Impact

An attacker could gain unauthorized access to user accounts or escalate privileges by obtaining the leaked root user access token from the logs, effectively impersonating legitimate users or administrators.

### Key diff

```
--- a/relay/mjproxy_handler.go
+++ b/relay/mjproxy_handler.go
@@ -473,7 +474,7 @@ func RelayMidjourneySubmit(c *gin.Context, relayInfo *relaycommon.RelayInfo) *dt
 			c.Set("base_url", channel.GetBaseURL())
 			c.Set("channel_id", originTask.ChannelId)
 			c.Request.Header.Set("Authorization", fmt.Sprintf("Bearer %s", channel.Key))
-			log.Printf("检测到此操作为放大、变换、重绘，获取原channel信息: %s,%s", strconv.Itoa(originTask.ChannelId), channel.GetBaseURL())
+			logger.LogDebug(c, "Midjourney action uses origin channel: id=%s, base_url=%s", strconv.Itoa(originTask.ChannelId), channel.GetBaseURL())
 		}
 		midjRequest.Prompt = originTask.Prompt
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies logging statements to use formatted logging functions directly (e.g., `logger.LogDebug(c, "format string", args...)`) instead of pre-formatting with `fmt.Sprintf`. This change, while seemingly minor, likely prevents sensitive data from being explicitly included in log messages, or it's a precursor to a logging library change that handles sensitive data more securely.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 8 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, Go, Python, C#, JavaScript
> → GHSA-cmxv-58fp-fm3g
> → GHSA-pm7q-rjjx-979p
> → GHSA-f7j3-774f-rfhj
> → GHSA-vh4v-2xq2-g5cg
> → GHSA-q62h-354g-5r85
> → GHSA-pf56-329r-95rw
> → GHSA-p2f4-r6v6-j797

## GHSA-73wf-9vmv-5pv9 · glances

2026-08-17 · Python · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The previous fix for CVE-2026-32608 was incomplete. It only sanitized top-level string values in the Mustache dictionary, failing to sanitize nested values within lists or dictionaries. Specifically, process 'cmdline' values, which are lists of arguments, could contain unsanitized shell operators, allowing an attacker to inject commands.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary OS commands into action command lines, leading to remote code execution on the system running Glances.

### Key diff

```
-    for k, v in mustache_dict.items():
-        if isinstance(v, str):
-            for op in _SHELL_OPERATORS:
-                v = v.replace(op, ' ')
-            safe[k] = v
-        else:
-            safe[k] = v
-    return safe
+    return {k: _sanitize_value(v) for k, v in mustache_dict.items()}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new recursive sanitization function, `_sanitize_value`, which walks through strings, lists, tuples, and dictionaries to replace shell operators with spaces. The `_sanitize_mustache_dict` function now uses this recursive sanitizer, ensuring that all nested attacker-controlled values are properly sanitized before being rendered into action command lines.

## GHSA-p28p-j94q-pg32 · org.http4k:http4k-security-digest

2026-08-17 · Java · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The `DigestAuthProvider.verify` method did not include the request URI in its verification process for Digest authentication. This allowed an attacker to reuse a valid Digest authentication header from one URI on a different URI, bypassing authentication for the second URI.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass authentication checks for protected resources by replaying a valid Digest authentication header obtained for a different, potentially less sensitive, URI.

### Key diff

```
--- a/core/security/digest/src/main/kotlin/org/http4k/security/digest/DigestAuthProvider.kt
+++ b/core/security/digest/src/main/kotlin/org/http4k/security/digest/DigestAuthProvider.kt
@@ -34,12 +34,13 @@ class DigestAuthProvider(
         .header(digestMode.authHeaderName)
         ?.let { DigestCredential.fromHeader(it) }
 
-    fun verify(credentials: DigestCredential, method: Method): Boolean {
+    fun verify(credentials: DigestCredential, method: Method, requestUri: String): Boolean {
         val digestEncoder = DigestEncoder(MessageDigest.getInstance(algorithm))
 
         // verify credentials pertain to this provider
         if (credentials.algorithm != null && credentials.algorithm != algorithm) return false
         if (credentials.realm != realm) return false
+        if (credentials.digestUri != requestUri) return false
         if (!nonceVerifier(credentials.nonce)) return false
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `DigestAuthProvider.verify` method to include the `requestUri` as a parameter and adds a check to ensure that the `credentials.digestUri` matches the actual `requestUri`. The `ServerFilters.DigestAuth` filter was also updated to pass the request URI to the verification method.

## GHSA-2qj4-mmr9-4v2f · io.netty:netty-transport-sctp

2026-08-17 · Java · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The Netty SOCKS and MQTT encoders did not properly validate the length of certain fields (like address, username, password, topic name) before writing them to a byte buffer. An attacker could send oversized values, causing the encoder to write more data than expected, leading to excessive memory allocation and potential memory exhaustion.

### Impact

An attacker could send specially crafted messages to a Netty server, causing it to allocate large amounts of memory and potentially crash due to an OutOfMemoryError, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/codec-socks/src/main/java/io/netty/handler/codec/socksx/v5/Socks5ClientEncoder.java
+++ b/codec-socks/src/main/java/io/netty/handler/codec/socksx/v5/Socks5ClientEncoder.java
@@ -80,7 +80,7 @@ private static void encodeAuthMethodRequest(Socks5InitialRequest msg, ByteBuf ou
 
         final List<Socks5AuthMethod> authMethods = msg.authMethods();
         final int numAuthMethods = authMethods.size();
-        out.writeByte(numAuthMethods);
+        writeFieldLength(out, numAuthMethods);
 
         if (authMethods instanceof RandomAccess) {
             for (int i = 0; i < numAuthMethods; i ++) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces length checks for various fields in SOCKS and MQTT encoders. For SOCKS, it ensures field lengths do not exceed 255 bytes and prevents null bytes in SOCKS4 fields. For MQTT, it adds validation for client identifiers, will topics, user names, and publish topic names, throwing exceptions for invalid lengths or characters.

## GHSA-prg7-hcfm-mfcr · sqlparse

2026-08-17 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from inefficient regex handling of dollar-quoted SQL literals and multiline comments. The lexer used a lazy dot-all quantifier (`[\s\S]*?`) terminated by a backreference or a literal closing sequence. When presented with many unique, unmatched opening delimiters, each regex attempt would rescan to the end of the remaining input, leading to O(n^2) complexity.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious SQL query containing many unclosed dollar-quoted literals or multiline comments. Processing this input would consume excessive CPU resources, leading to a denial of service for the application using the `sqlparse` library.

### Key diff

```
--- a/sqlparse/lexer.py
+++ b/sqlparse/lexer.py
@@ -132,8 +132,16 @@ def get_tokens(self, text, encoding=None):
         else:
             raise TypeError(f"Expected text or file-like object, got {type(text)!r}")
 
+        delimited_spans = keywords.find_delimited_spans(text)
+
         iterable = enumerate(text)
         for pos, char in iterable:
+            if pos in delimited_spans:
+                end, ttype = delimited_spans[pos]
+                yield ttype, text[pos:end]
+                consume(iterable, end - pos - 1)
+                continue
+
             for rexmatch, action in self._SQL_REGEX:
                 m = rexmatch(text, pos)
 
--- a/sqlparse/keywords.py
+++ b/sqlparse/keywords.py
@@ -13,12 +16,64 @@
 PROCESS_AS_KEYWORD = object()
 
 
+# Dollar-quoted literals (`$tag$...$tag$`) and multiline comments
+# (`/*...*/`, `/*+...*/`) used to be matched with per-position regexes
+# using a lazy dot-all quantifier (`[\s\S]*?`) terminated by a
+# backreference or a literal delimiter. Applied at every text position by
+# the lexer loop below, that shape is O(n^2) on adversarial input with
+# many unclosed openers, since each failed attempt re-scans to the end of
+# the remaining text (GHSA-prg7-hcfm-mfcr). They are resolved instead in
+# a single linear pass by find_delimited_spans().
+_DOLLAR_QUOTE_DELIM = re.compile(r'\$(?:[_A-ZÀ-Ü]\w*)?$', re.IGNORECASE | re.UNICODE)
+_DOLLAR_QUOTE_OPENER_OK = re.compile(r'(?<![\w"$])', re.UNICODE)
+_COMMENT_HINT_OPEN = re.compile(r'/\*\+')
+_COMMENT_OPEN = re.compile(r'/\*(?!\+)')
+_COMMENT_CLOSE = re.compile(r'\*/')
+
+
+def find_delimited_spans(text):
+    """Locate dollar-quoted literals and multiline comments in `text`.
+
+    Returns a dict mapping each span's start offset to
+    (end offset, token type).
+    """
+    has_dollar = '$' in text
+    # A comment can only ever open on "/*"; without it "*/" alone can
+    # never pair with anything, so gating on "/*" alone is sufficient to
+    # skip all three comment-related regex passes below.
+    has_comment_open = '/*' in text
+    if not has_dollar and not has_comment_open:
+        return {}
+
+    occurrences = []
+    if has_dollar:
+        for m in _DOLLAR_QUOTE_DELIM.finditer(text):
+            tag = m.group()
+            can_open = _DOLLAR_QUOTE_OPENER_OK.match(text, m.start()) is not None
+            occurrences.append(
+                _DelimiterOccurrence(m.start(), m.end(), tag, can_open, True, tokens.Literal))
+    if has_comment_open:
+        for m in _COMMENT_HINT_OPEN.finditer(text):
+            occurrences.append(_DelimiterOccurrence(
+                m.start(), m.end(), 'C', True, False,
+                tokens.Comment.Multiline.Hint))
+        for m in _COMMENT_OPEN.finditer(text):
+            occurrences.append(_DelimiterOccurrence(
+                m.start(), m.end(), 'C', True, False,
+                tokens.Comment.Multiline))
+        for m in _COMMENT_CLOSE.finditer(text):
+            occurrences.append(
+                _DelimiterOccurrence(m.start(), m.end(), 'C', False, True, None))
+    occurrences.sort(key=lambda occ: occ.start)
+
+    spans = resolve_paired_delimiters(occurrences)
+    return {start: (end, ttype) for start, end, ttype in spans}
+
+
 SQL_REGEX = [
     (r'(--|# )\+.*?(\r\n|\r|\n|$)', tokens.Comment.Single.Hint),
-    (r'/\*\+[\s\S]*?\*/', tokens.Comment.Multiline.Hint),
+
     (r'(--|# ).*?(\r\n|\r|\n|$)', tokens.Comment.Single),
-    (r'/\*[\s\S]*?\*/', tokens.Comment.Multiline),
+
     (r'(\r\n|\r|\n)', tokens.Newline),
     (r'\s+?', tokens.Whitespace),
     (r'((?i)BEGIN|(?i)END)', PROCESS_AS_KEYWORD),
 
     (r"`(``|[^`])*`", tokens.Name),
     (r"´(´´|[^´])*´", tokens.Name),
-    (r'((?<![\w\"$])\$(?:[_A-ZÀ-Ü]\w*)?\$)[\s\S]*?\1', tokens.Literal),
+
     (r'\?', tokens.Name.Placeholder),
     (r'%(\(\w+\))?s', tokens.Name.Placeholder),
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the handling of dollar-quoted literals and multiline comments. Instead of using per-position regexes in the main lexer loop, a new function `find_delimited_spans` is introduced to locate these spans in a single linear pass. This pre-computation identifies the start and end of these tokens, allowing the main lexer to skip over them efficiently.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 141 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, Python, JavaScript, RUST, Java, C#, PHP, Ruby, Erlang

## GHSA-gqch-g4w5-7qcw · mlflow

2026-08-17 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The `CreateModelVersion` API endpoint in MLflow did not properly validate read permissions on the `run_id` or `model_id` referenced in the `source` field. While downstream artifact reads were gated, the initial creation of the model version did not enforce this check, allowing a user to link to another user's run/model artifacts without explicit read permission.

### Impact

An attacker could create a model version that points to artifacts belonging to another user's run or model. This would allow the attacker to bypass access controls and read sensitive artifacts from runs or models they do not have permission to access, effectively leading to unauthorized data disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/mlflow/server/auth/__init__.py
+++ b/mlflow/server/auth/__init__.py
@@ -2479,7 +2499,7 @@ def filter_list_review_queues(resp: Response) -> None:
     UpdateRegisteredModel: _validate_can_update_registered_model_or_prompt,
     RenameRegisteredModel: _validate_can_update_registered_model_or_prompt,
     GetLatestVersions: _validate_can_read_registered_model_or_prompt,
-    CreateModelVersion: _validate_can_update_registered_model_or_prompt,
+    CreateModelVersion: validate_can_create_model_version,
     GetModelVersion: _validate_can_read_registered_model_or_prompt,
     DeleteModelVersion: _validate_can_delete_registered_model_or_prompt,
     UpdateModelVersion: _validate_can_update_registered_model_or_prompt,
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new validation function, `validate_can_create_model_version`, specifically for the `CreateModelVersion` endpoint. This function now explicitly checks if the user has `READ` permission on the `run_id` or `model_id` specified in the request body, in addition to the existing update permission check on the registered model. This ensures consistency between create-time access and subsequent artifact-read gating.

## GHSA-m44r-7c5h-m6mj · @medplum/core

2026-08-17 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `OPEN_REDIRECT→PHISHING`

### Root cause

The application improperly validated redirect URIs in the external authentication callback. It allowed partial matches for redirect URIs, which could be exploited by an attacker to redirect authorization codes to a malicious domain if the legitimate redirect URI was a prefix of the attacker's controlled URI.

### Impact

An attacker could intercept authorization codes, potentially leading to account takeover or unauthorized access to user data by exchanging the stolen code for an access token.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/server/src/auth/external.ts
+++ b/packages/server/src/auth/external.ts
-    const redirectUri = getClientRedirectUri(client, body.redirectUri, true);
-    if (!redirectUri) {
-      sendOutcome(res, badRequest('Invalid redirect URI'));
-      return;
-    }
-    const exactRedirectUri = getClientRedirectUri(client, redirectUri);
-    if (!exactRedirectUri) {
+    let redirectUri = getClientRedirectUri(client, body.redirectUri);
+
+    if (!redirectUri && (await isDangerousRedirectUriPartialMatchAllowed(client))) {
+      redirectUri = getClientRedirectUri(client, body.redirectUri, true);
+    }
+
+    if (!redirectUri) {
+      sendOutcome(res, badRequest('Invalid redirect URI'));
+      return;
+    }
```

### Fix summary

The patch tightens the redirect URI validation logic. Partial matching is now only allowed if explicitly enabled via a 'allow-dangerous-redirect' project setting. Additionally, the partial matching logic itself was improved to ensure that only the path, not the entire origin, can be partially matched, preventing redirection to arbitrary origins.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 16 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, JavaScript, Python, PHP
> → GHSA-x3f4-v83f-7wp2
> → GHSA-8f24-v5vv-gm5j
> → GHSA-3f6h-2hrp-w5wx

## GHSA-3p64-6gvh-82v5 · mlflow

2026-08-17 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The 'LogInputs' and 'LogOutputs' endpoints were not explicitly included in the authorization checks for updating a run. This oversight meant that while other run-related update operations were protected by 'validate_can_update_run', these specific endpoints could be accessed without proper authorization.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass the per-run UPDATE authorization, allowing them to modify the inputs and outputs associated with an MLflow run even if they did not have the necessary permissions to update the run itself.

### Key diff

```
--- a/mlflow/server/auth/__init__.py
+++ b/mlflow/server/auth/__init__.py
@@ -2461,7 +2463,9 @@ def filter_list_review_queues(resp: Response) -> None:
     UpdateRun: validate_can_update_run,
     LogMetric: validate_can_update_run,
     LogBatch: validate_can_update_run,
+    LogInputs: validate_can_update_run,
     LogModel: validate_can_update_run,
+    LogOutputs: validate_can_update_run,
     SetTag: validate_can_update_run,
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds 'LogInputs' and 'LogOutputs' to the dictionary mapping API requests to their respective authorization validation functions. Specifically, it assigns 'validate_can_update_run' to both 'LogInputs' and 'LogOutputs', ensuring that these endpoints now require proper authorization before allowing modifications.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 93 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, Ruby, JavaScript, Python, PHP, Java, RUST, Erlang, C#

## GHSA-vxxm-wwqh-mh47 · org.http4k:http4k-security-digest

2026-08-17 · Java · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `WEAK_CRYPTO→HASH`

### Root cause

The `DigestAuthProvider.verify` method hardcoded the algorithm to MD5 instead of using the algorithm configured for the provider. This meant that even if a stronger algorithm was specified during provider instantiation, the verification process would always use MD5, potentially weakening the security of the digest authentication.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially bypass the digest authentication if they could force the use of a weaker algorithm (MD5) or exploit known weaknesses in MD5, even when the system was configured to use a stronger algorithm.

### Key diff

```
--- a/core/security/digest/src/main/kotlin/org/http4k/security/digest/DigestAuthProvider.kt
+++ b/core/security/digest/src/main/kotlin/org/http4k/security/digest/DigestAuthProvider.kt
@@ -35,7 +35,7 @@ class DigestAuthProvider(
         ?.let { DigestCredential.fromHeader(it) }
 
     fun verify(credentials: DigestCredential, method: Method): Boolean {
-        val digestEncoder = DigestEncoder(MessageDigest.getInstance("MD5"))
+        val digestEncoder = DigestEncoder(MessageDigest.getInstance(algorithm))
 
         // verify credentials pertain to this provider
         if (credentials.algorithm != null && credentials.algorithm != algorithm) return false
```

### Fix summary

The patch changes the `DigestAuthProvider.verify` method to use the `algorithm` property of the `DigestAuthProvider` instance when creating the `DigestEncoder`. This ensures that the configured algorithm is respected during the verification process.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 4 occurrences**
> Languages: RUST, JavaScript, Go, Java
> → GHSA-3g92-f9ch-qjcm
> → GHSA-39pv-4j6c-2g6v
> → GHSA-hg5r-vq93-9fv6

🔗 [View commit](https://github.com/http4k/http4k/commit/65d23d99fc)

## GHSA-8qf9-62x2-82pp · chrome-devtools-mcp

2026-08-17 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.1 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The `validatePath` function did not canonicalize symbolic links before checking if a requested file path was within allowed workspace roots. An attacker could use symlinks to bypass the path validation and access files outside the intended directories.

### Impact

An attacker could read or write arbitrary files on the system, depending on the operations performed after path validation, by crafting a path that uses symbolic links to escape the restricted directories.

### Key diff

```
-	    const absolutePath = path.resolve(filePath);
-	    for (const root of roots) {
-	      const rootPath = path.resolve(fileURLToPath(root.uri));
+	    let canonicalPath: string;
+	    try {
+	      canonicalPath = await resolveCanonicalPath(filePath);
+	    } catch (err) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `validatePath` function to use `fsPromises.realpath` to resolve the canonical path of both the requested file and the configured root directories. This ensures that all symbolic links are resolved before path validation, preventing bypasses.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 72 occurrences**

## GHSA-2jp7-wwpg-3p9w · ep_etherpad-lite

2026-08-17 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because attribute-pool values, which can contain user-controlled content, were directly interpolated into HTML attributes in the exported HTML without proper escaping. This allowed an attacker to inject malicious JavaScript.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary JavaScript into the exported HTML, leading to stored Cross-Site Scripting (XSS). This could allow them to steal user cookies, deface the page, or perform actions on behalf of the user.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/node/utils/ExportHtml.ts
+++ b/src/node/utils/ExportHtml.ts
@@ -63,7 +63,12 @@ const getHTMLFromAtext = async (pad:PadType, atext: AText, authorColors?: string
     // like <span data-tag="value">
     hooks.aCallAll('exportHtmlAdditionalTagsWithData', pad).then((newProps: string[]) => {
       newProps.forEach((prop) => {
-        tags.push(`span data-${prop[0]}="${prop[1]}"`);
+        // Attribute names/values here originate from the pad's attribute pool
+        // (user content), so escape the value and constrain the data-* name
+        // before interpolating, consistent with the escaping already applied to
+        // exported URLs and text below.
+        const dataName = String(prop[0]).replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9_-]/g, '');
+        tags.push(`span data-${dataName}="${Security.escapeHTMLAttribute(String(prop[1]))}"`);
         props.push(prop);
       });
     }),
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by sanitizing the attribute names and HTML-escaping the attribute values before they are interpolated into the `data-*` attributes in the exported HTML. It uses `String(prop[0]).replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9_-]/g, '')` for attribute names and `Security.escapeHTMLAttribute(String(prop[1]))` for attribute values.

## GHSA-59fj-m2j6-hcxh · glances

2026-08-17 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The previous fix for CVE-2026-53925 (GHSA-3vwc-qwhc-3mj7) incompletely addressed command injection. While it disabled shell operators for 'AMP commands', it failed to do so for 'on-alert action commands' which are also sourced from the same configuration file. This oversight allowed an attacker to inject shell operators into these on-alert commands.

### Impact

An attacker able to modify the `glances.conf` file could execute arbitrary commands on the system, chain commands, or write to arbitrary files, leading to remote code execution.

### Key diff

```
--- a/glances/actions.py
+++ b/glances/actions.py
@@ -125,7 +141,7 @@ def run(self, stat_name, criticality, commands, repeat, mustache_dict=None):
             # Execute the action
             logger.info(f"Action triggered for {stat_name} ({criticality}): {cmd_full})")
             try:
-                ret = secure_popen(cmd_full)
+                ret = secure_popen(cmd_full, allow_operators=self.allow_operators())
             except OSError as e:
                 logger.error(f"Action error for {stat_name} ({criticality}): {e})")
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an `allow_operators` method that checks the `disable_config_exec` argument. This method is then passed to the `secure_popen` function when executing on-alert action commands, ensuring that shell operators are disabled if the configuration execution is meant to be restricted.

## GHSA-92hr-gmr6-h8cp · ep_etherpad-lite

2026-08-17 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The system suffered from several weaknesses: predictable random number generation for tokens, a login mechanism vulnerable to timing attacks, insufficient validation of plugin dependency names leading to path traversal, and improper handling of API request parameters which could lead to unexpected behavior or bypasses.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially enumerate valid usernames, brute-force tokens, read arbitrary files via path traversal in plugin dependencies, or manipulate API requests to achieve unintended actions.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/node/security/OAuth2Provider.ts
+++ b/src/node/security/OAuth2Provider.ts
@@ -171,21 +186,28 @@ export const expressCreateServer = async (hookName: string, args: ArgsExpressTyp
                             admin: boolean;
                         }
                     }
-                    const usersArray1 = Object.keys(users).map((username) => ({
-                        username,
-                        ...users[username]
-                    }));
-                    const account = usersArray1.find((user) => user.username === login as unknown as string && user.password === password as unknown as string);
+                    const loginStr = String(login ?? '');
+                    const passwordStr = String(password ?? '');
+                    const user = Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(users, loginStr)
+                        ? users[loginStr] : undefined;
+                    const passwordOk = user != null &&
+                        constantTimeEquals(passwordStr, String(user.password));
+                    const account = passwordOk ? {username: loginStr, ...user} : undefined;
                     if (!account) {
+                        await new Promise((resolve) =>
+                            setTimeout(resolve, OAUTH_LOGIN_FAILURE_DELAY_MS));
                         res.setHeader('Content-Type', 'application/json');
                         res.end(JSON.stringify({error: "Invalid login"}));
+                        break;
                     }
 
-                    if (account) {
-                        await oidc.interactionFinished(req, res, {
-                            login: {accountId: account.username}
-                        }, {mergeWithLastSubmission: false});
-                    }
+                    await oidc.interactionFinished(req, res, {
+                        login: {accountId: account.username}
+                    }, {mergeWithLastSubmission: false});
                     break;
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `crypto.getRandomValues` for generating random strings, implements `crypto.timingSafeEqual` and a fixed delay for login attempts to mitigate timing attacks, adds validation for plugin dependency names to prevent path traversal, and refines API request parameter merging to ensure correct precedence and prevent unintended overrides.

## GHSA-f2ff-p2ww-7p4p · sqlparse

2026-08-17 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an inefficient algorithm in the `group_comments` function. When processing SQL statements composed solely of single-line comments, the function would repeatedly rescan the entire remaining list of tokens for each comment, leading to a quadratic time complexity (O(n^2)) instead of the expected linear (O(n)).

### Impact

An attacker could craft a SQL query consisting of a large number of single-line comments. Processing this query would consume excessive CPU resources, potentially leading to a denial of service for applications using the `sqlparse` library.

### Key diff

```
--- a/sqlparse/engine/grouping.py
+++ b/sqlparse/engine/grouping.py
@@ -334,9 +334,14 @@ def group_comments(tlist):
     while token:
         eidx, end = tlist.token_not_matching(
             lambda tk: imt(tk, t=T.Comment) or tk.is_newline, idx=tidx)
-        if end is not None:
-            eidx, end = tlist.token_prev(eidx, skip_ws=False)
-            tlist.group_tokens(sql.Comment, tidx, eidx)
+        if end is None:
+            # From tidx onward everything is comment/newline: there is no
+            # terminator to group against, and every later start would hit
+            # the same dead end. Stop instead of re-scanning the tail once
+            # per remaining comment token (which is O(n**2)).
+            break
+        eidx, end = tlist.token_prev(eidx, skip_ws=False)
+        tlist.group_tokens(sql.Comment, tidx, eidx)
 
         tidx, token = tlist.token_next_by(t=T.Comment, idx=tidx)
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the quadratic complexity by introducing a check within the `group_comments` function. If it detects that all remaining tokens from the current position onward are comments or newlines, it breaks out of the loop. This prevents redundant rescanning and ensures linear time complexity for comment-only inputs.

## GHSA-m6jg-wr9m-cg2f · gitlab.com/uniget-org/cli

2026-08-17 · Go · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application allowed users to specify hook file paths without proper validation, making it possible to use path traversal sequences (e.g., `../`) to access or modify files outside the intended hook directories. Additionally, the application did not prevent the use of symbolic links, which could be exploited to bypass path checks.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially read, write, or delete arbitrary files on the system by manipulating hook file paths. This could lead to privilege escalation, data exfiltration, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/cmd/uniget/hooks.go
+++ b/cmd/uniget/hooks.go
@@ -164,6 +165,14 @@ var removeHooksCmd = &cobra.Command{
 			hookFile = postUninstallHooksDir + "/" + hookFileName
 		}
 
+ 		hookFileAbs, err := filepath.Abs(hookFile)
+ 		if err != nil {
+ 			return fmt.Errorf("unable to get absolute path of hook file %s: %w", hookFile, err)
+ 		}
+ 		if !strings.HasPrefix(hookFileAbs, hooksDir) {
+ 			return fmt.Errorf("hook file %s is outside of hookDir %s", hookFile, hooksDir)
+ 		}
+
 		if !fileExists(hookFile) {
 			return fmt.Errorf("hook file does not exist: %s", hookFile)
 		}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces checks to ensure that hook file paths, after being resolved to their absolute form, remain within the designated `hooksDir`. It also explicitly disallows symbolic links for hook files to prevent directory escape via symlink following. The `editHooksCmd` also now correctly parses the editor command to prevent command injection.

## GHSA-pwgv-4x5q-6m9f · sqlparse

2026-08-17 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The original implementation of `TokenList.__init__` and `group_tokens` would repeatedly call `str(self)` or `str(start)` respectively. When dealing with deeply nested or large token lists, this would cause the entire subtree's value to be re-materialized as a string in an O(subtree) operation for each group, leading to excessive CPU consumption.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a specially designed SQL query that, when parsed by `sqlparse`, would consume a disproportionate amount of CPU resources, leading to a denial of service for the application or system processing the query.

### Key diff

```
--- a/sqlparse/sql.py
+++ b/sqlparse/sql.py
@@ -164,7 +164,7 @@ class TokenList(Token):
     def __init__(self, tokens=None):
         self.tokens = tokens or []
         [setattr(token, 'parent', self) for token in self.tokens]
-        super().__init__(None, str(self))
+        super().__init__(None, ''.join(token.value for token in self.tokens))
         self.is_group = True
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies `TokenList.__init__` to use `''.join(token.value for token in self.tokens)` instead of `str(self)`, and `group_tokens` to append new token values using `grp.value += ''.join(token.value for token in subtokens)`. This avoids the repeated and expensive re-materialization of the entire group's string value, instead building it incrementally or efficiently.

## GHSA-qcpp-8x79-hhp3 · glances

2026-08-17 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The previous sanitization logic for shell operators did not account for the reconstruction of multi-character operators (like '&&') when two single-character operators (like '&') were present at the boundary of two adjacent, unsanitized Mustache variables. This allowed an attacker to bypass the existing sanitizer by splitting a shell operator across two input fields.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary command execution on the system where Glances is running, potentially leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/glances/actions.py
+++ b/glances/actions.py
@@ -22,7 +22,18 @@
 
 # Characters that secure_popen interprets as shell operators.
 # Mustache-rendered values must not contain these to prevent command injection.
-_SHELL_OPERATORS = ('&&', '|', '>>', '>')
+# ... (comment explaining the change)
+_SHELL_OPERATORS = ('&&', '|', '>>', '>', '&')
```

### Fix summary

The patch extends the list of shell operators to be sanitized to include the lone '&' character. This prevents the reconstruction of multi-character shell operators like '&&' when two adjacent Mustache variables are rendered, as both sides of the potential operator are now stripped.
